much better upper bounds can be obtained at the price of a worse gap function. This is because
considering bigger walloids may provide us with enough space for proving that more complicated
embedding pairs are “absorbed” by simpler ones which would allow us to discard some of the members
of the obstructing set WH for H-treewidth.

    Our conjecture is that the bound 22            can be considerably improved to one that is polynomial
in h. However, we do not believe that this may become possible without paying the price of a worse
gap function in our parametric equivalences.

Only few obstructions are enough. Let O = ⟨Ok ⟩k∈N be some parametric family of antichains
for the minor relation. We say that O defines the parameter p if p(G) is the minimum k such that
G excludes all graphs in Ok as minors. We would like to suggest the following interpretation of our
obstructing sets (or better, the universal obstructions obtained from their minimizations) for the
parameters H-tw, H-td, and apexH : We first define

                                     T WkH    = {G | H-tw(G) ≤ k},                                      (34)

and recall the analogous definitions of the graph classes T DkH and APkH given in (29) and (21)
respectively. Using the terminology above, it holds that

                  pobs(T WkH ) = H-tw, pobs(T DkH ) = H-td, and      pobs(APkH ) = apexH .




                                                    123
Unfortunately, the sizes of the parametric families obs(T WkH ), obs(T DkH ), and obs(APkH ) grow fast as
k increases. For instance, in the base case where H = ∅, where we obtain the parameters tw/td/vc,24
exponential lower bounds to the number of obstructions are known, see [58] for tw, [16] for td, and [14]
for vc. Also, as we already mentioned in the beginnings of Section 12 and Subsection 12.3 there are
explicit upper bounds for the sizes of obs(T DkH ) and obs(APkH ) (to our knowledge no explicit bound
to the size of the members of obs(T WkH ) is known). Given the current state of the art, it appears
rather hopeless to expect that some precise characterization of these obstruction sets may be found.
Our obstructing sets suggest that this becomes possible if we drop our expectations and only ask for
parametric equivalence. In fact, our results imply that, no matter the value of k, we may discard all but
  O(ℓ(h2 ))
22          of the members of obs(T WkH )/obs(T DkH )/obs(APkH ) and still have the following parametric
families of antichains
                                                                                  apexH
                OH-tw = ⟨OkH-tw ⟩k∈N , OH-td = ⟨OkH-td ⟩k∈N and OapexH = ⟨Ok              ⟩k∈N ,

where the parameters defined by these sets are equivalent to H-tw, H-td, and apexH . This means that
         O(ℓ(h2 ))
“only” 22        obstructions are enough for determining the parametric behavior of H-tw, H-td, and
apexH , for any choice of the class H. An interesting question is whether by including more (but still not
too many) obstructions in the obstruction sets above one can obtain better parametric (preferably with
polynomial or even linear gaps) estimations for the corresponding parameters. Is there an interpretation
of the constant-factor approximation algorithms in [32] via parametric graph exclusion?

Strict and strong brambles. As we have seen in Corollary 11.9, for every minor-closed class H, the
existence of a strict H-bramble of order f11.9 (h, k) in a graph implies the existence of a 1/2-H-bramble
of order k. The function f11.9 (h, k) here again depends on our general gap function and is single
exponential in k. We would certainly like to improve this and we believe that it is indeed possible. The
reason is that the 1/2-H-brambles found by our proof are of very particular nature: they stem directly
from our walloids and their construction requires the full machinery of our proof and the supporting
results from [39] and [77]. However, 1/2-H-brambles may not only be certified by walloids and we
cannot exclude the existence of better bounds. For instance, when H has some planar obstruction,
that is when H-tw = tw + Θh (1), much better dependencies are known. In particular, Hatzel, Komosa,
Pilipczuk, and Sorge proved in [28] that the existence of a 1/2-bramble of order k is implied by a
bramble of order Ω(k 2 · polylog(k)) (see also [27, 44] for related results). Whether these bounds can be
extended for other choices of H is an open question. For this, even a polynomial gap for the case where
H is the class of planar graphs would be interesting. Another direction is to pursue better dependencies
by relaxing the half-integrality, for instance by asking for a 1/3-H-bramble or a 1/4-H-bramble where
no vertex is used by more than 3 or 4 bramble elements. Is there such a relaxation that may lead to a
polynomial dependency for all Hs?

Special families of H’s. Our work leaves the project of precisely determining Wmin         H for particular
instantiations of the class H as an open problem. Are there additional properties for H that may
facilitate such a description? As a first step in this direction, in [56], we focus on a particular family of
H’s, namely those whose obstructions are all Kuratowski connected and which contain at least one
obstruction that is a minor of a shallow-vortex grid. Providing the definitions of these conditions escape
the purposes of this paper (for the interested reader, the definitions can be found in [70] and [76]).
We wish to mention that when these conditions are imposed on H, the obstructing sets are much
simpler: They are one or two walloids capturing surfaces (that is, these walloids do not have flower
segments) and, using our terminology, are those which are generated by pairs (Σ, B) where B = ∅,
and a third type of walloid which is generated by pairs of the form (Σ(0,0) , B∗ ) where B∗ = {⟨Z, ⟨y⟩⟩},
Z ∈ obs(H), and y is an arbitrary vertex of Z.
 24 we use vc for the vertex cover number of a graph.
